## Workflow Start
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-06T10:20:21Z
**Event**: WORKFLOW_STARTED
**Scope**: mvp
**Request**: /aidlc Build the Asset Tracker MVP from the approved Asset Tracker MVP Development Workbook, the POC-vs-MVP gap analysis, the decision brief, and the fully approved 17-item decision set. The MVP adds actor-attributed append-only audit history, previous-holder preservation via last_assigned_to, lost-asset recovery (lost to in-stock), an assigned to discarded transition that resolves the assignment inline, five exclusive status buckets with Active Inventory and Total Records computed by one shared counting function and filter builder, case-insensitive category-scoped serial uniqueness via a functional index with a concurrency-safe application-level fallback, CSV export with formula-injection protection, and an amendment to workbook section 4.1 and section 21.1 Scenario 3 recording Returned as a distinct resting state.

## Questions: Rough Mockups — Batch 1 of 1
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T02:34:31Z
**User Input**: "Q1: A. Eight screens: five MVP surfaces plus three POC screens | Q2: A. Registration never assigns — remove the field | Q3: B. A confirmation dialog that collects the reason | Q4: B. Inline validation plus a submit-time collision warning | Q5: B. Two headline tiles plus the five buckets below | Q6: A. A list of holders, each expandable to their assets | Q7: A. The full append-only history, newest first, with the actor on every entry | Q8: A. Persistent actor indicator in the header, marked provisional | Q9: A. Four-item top navigation: Dashboard, Assets, Holders, plus New asset | Q10: A. Desktop only, and stated as a deferral | Q11: A. WCAG 2.1 AA | Q12: A. Stay inside the POC's existing visual vocabulary | Assumptions: A. Accept assumptions"

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T02:34:35Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: rough-mockups
**Details**: Q1: A. Eight screens: five MVP surfaces plus three POC screens; Q2: A. Registration never assigns — remove the field; Q3: B. A confirmation dialog that collects the reason; Q4: B. Inline validation plus a submit-time collision warning; Q5: B. Two headline tiles plus the five buckets below; Q6: A. A list of holders, each expandable to their assets; Q7: A. The full append-only history, newest first, with the actor on every entry; Q8: A. Persistent actor indicator in the header, marked provisional; Q9: A. Four-item top navigation: Dashboard, Assets, Holders, plus New asset; Q10: A. Desktop only, and stated as a deferral; Q11: A. WCAG 2.1 AA; Q12: A. Stay inside the POC's existing visual vocabulary; Assumption Confirmation: A. Accept assumptions

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:04:53Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q1: D — the actor-trust rule plus its consequences for the release (audit actor field, actor on the history panel, the read-only Acting as indicator, and CSRF protection if the mechanism is a browser-attached credential). Q2: D — the mechanism is deferred, and the requirements additionally state what must be true of whichever mechanism is chosen (a stable, displayable identity; no client-settable field or header as the source).

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:06:43Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:06:51Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q3: B — deleting an asset row is forbidden, the audit row's asset reference stays required, and a deletion attempt is refused with a clear, machine-readable error rather than a bare database failure. Q4: D — the measure covers every audit entry in the database at any moment, the number of entries lacking an actor must be zero, the backfilled population is excluded, and it is evaluated both as a standing assertion on each write path and as a single countable check over the whole table. Q5: B — it counts asset rows, Active Inventory covers in-stock/assigned/returned/lost, Total Records covers all five, a discarded asset is in Total Records but not Active Inventory, and the assignee directory's own counts are specified (a holder row is a distinct display holder, the count line excludes the Unassigned row, and a holder's per-bucket chips sum to that holder's row count).

## Human Turn
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:09:36Z
**Event**: HUMAN_TURN

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:09:45Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q6: A with detail — predictable filename assets-export.csv, content type text/csv; charset=utf-8, UTF-8 encoding, uncapped at the approved medium scale, exports all rows matching the current list filters and never silently truncates, performance validated against representative upper-end data, approved column names and order preserved, no embedded filter metadata, header-only file for zero matching rows, formula-injection sanitisation on every cell. Q7: A with detail — collision is a same-category serial/key match after trimming surrounding whitespace and case-insensitive comparison, the edited asset excluded from its own check, both the inline warning and the submit-time block identify the existing asset and link to it and explain the next steps, no override to create a duplicate, application comparison and database functional index share exactly the same normalisation, database authoritative under concurrent requests. Q8: D — a save appends one history entry naming the changed fields and their previous values, a save that changes nothing appends no entry, and the asset's status and its current and last holder cannot be changed by an edit.

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:12:03Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q9: B — the seven wireframe accessibility commitments applied to the new surfaces with a skip link as the first focusable element on every screen, plus a per-screen statement of heading level, primary landmark regions, and keyboard entry point. Q10: no fixed ceilings are invented without a measured baseline; the requirements establish measurable performance acceptance at the confirmed planning scale and require measuring dashboard count retrieval and rendering, assignee directory grouping and retrieval, filtered asset-list retrieval, CSV generation for all matching rows, write and audit-entry completion time, and SQLite lock-wait behaviour under concurrent writes; percentiles, export generation time, and write latency are recorded; the export must not silently truncate; lock contention follows the approved retryable SQLITE_BUSY behaviour; explicit numerical acceptance ceilings are set from the measured baseline and approved by the Product Owner before validation is treated as passed, and are not presented as existing commitments. Q11: a failure is an audit row that cannot be given the identifiable system actor while preserving its original content and traceability; the migration preserves every original record, is idempotent, counts and reports failures with row identifiers and reasons, fails the migration and prevents deployment if any row fails, rolls back the backfill transaction, never silently skips or partially modifies failed rows, and a human investigates and resolves before a retry.

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T11:53:01Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q14: D — the requirement states the display-holder rule as the single definition used by the directory (current holder, else preserved last holder, else no holder), states that the Unassigned row contains exactly the assets never held by anybody, and states the consequence for the release's two new transitions: a recovered asset and a discarded asset are each counted under their preserved last holder and not in Unassigned, because both retain last_assigned_to. Q15: C — the two omitted edges are recorded as deferred, and the requirements state explicitly that they remain approved edges of the transition table, so a later reader sees that the difference is the release boundary and not the approved design. Q16: C — the requirements restate the normalised uniqueness rule as its own requirement and record FR1.3's traceability as changed behaviour under an approved amendment, citing the workbook §4.1 amendment surface that carries the uniqueness policy, without characterising it as a retirement. Q17: A — an audit entry lacks an actor when its actor field is NULL or empty; the system sentinel is a populated actor value, so a backfilled row counts as having an actor, and the backfilled population is excluded from the metric's population as the §24 amendment requires.

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T12:05:34Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Details**: Q18: C — the system actor value IS the marker that identifies the excluded backfilled population, and a row the backfill failed to complete — one whose actor is still NULL or empty — IS inside the measured population, because it was not successfully backfilled and therefore cannot be identified as pre-MVP history. Q19: A — the stored serial or key is the user's input as typed, and normalisation (trimming and case folding) is applied only in the comparison predicate and in the functional index expression; the system displays and exports the value as the user supplied it. Q20: C — the requirement states the dataset by its properties (row count at the top of the confirmed medium scale band, distribution across all five status buckets, a holder set large enough to exercise the directory's grouping) and additionally requires the dataset's row count, bucket distribution, and holder count to be recorded alongside the measured results. Q6's answer label was corrected from A to B, since the specified detail is uncapped and supersedes option A's capping clause.

## Gate Rejected
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T12:50:46Z
**Event**: GATE_REJECTED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Feedback**: Advisory review returned READY with 3 Major, 4 Minor and 1 Note findings, all New. The human elected to fix all eight: R-1 restate FR11.1 against the wireframes' seven accessibility commitments; R-2 split FR1.7's permanent duplicate-serial failure from the transient busy condition; R-3 add the IMMEDIATE transaction, busy_timeout and migration-ordering requirements so standing condition C-1 is carried in full; R-4 state in FR2.2 that a request-supplied actor is ignored and the write still succeeds; R-5 correct the stale FR1-FR10 and C1-C10 navigation ranges; R-6 present the Attention panel as two separate whole-bucket counts; R-7 cite A13 and A16 from the requirements they underpin; R-8 state the audit-entry description rule once. No scope change, no new product decision, no reopening of the approved decision set.

---

**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T12:54:05Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Decision**: Consolidated summary of the Requirements Analysis stage, revised against all eight advisory-review findings (R-1 to R-8): FR11.1 restated against the wireframes' seven accessibility commitments; FR1.7 split into a permanent duplicate-serial failure and a transient busy condition; the IMMEDIATE transaction and busy_timeout requirements added as FR1.6a and the migration-ordering constraint added as NFR3.5a, so standing condition C-1 is carried in full; FR2.2 stating that a request-supplied actor is ignored and the write still succeeds; the stale navigation ranges corrected; the Attention panel restated as two separate whole-bucket counts; A13 and A16 cited from the requirements they underpin; and the audit-entry description rule stated once as FR2.8. No scope change, no new product decision, and no reopening of the approved decision set.

## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T13:02:55Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Decision**: Consolidated summary of the Requirements Analysis stage, revision 3: all eleven findings from the two advisory review passes are addressed. FR11.1 enumerates the wireframes' seven accessibility commitments; FR1.7 distinguishes the permanent duplicate-serial failure from the transient busy condition and names the driver codes SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE and SQLITE_BUSY; FR1.6a and NFR3.5a carry the IMMEDIATE transaction, busy_timeout and migration-ordering requirements so standing condition C-1 is held in full; FR2.2 states that a request-supplied actor is ignored and the write still succeeds; the navigation ranges are corrected; the Attention panel carries two separate whole-bucket counts; A13 and A16 are cited from the requirements they underpin; FR2.8 states the audit-entry description rule once and now enumerates the create action as well; and NFR3.2 names the SQLITE_BUSY detection rule. R-11, the orphaned A11 assumption, is accepted as an open tidiness note. No scope change, no new product decision, and no reopening of the approved decision set.
**Options**: Looks correct,Request changes
**Checkpoint**: Consolidated Summary Confirmation

## Gate Rejected
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T13:07:23Z
**Event**: GATE_REJECTED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Feedback**: The re-review confirmed R-9 and R-10 resolved and R-11 honestly accepted as an open note, and returned one new Minor finding, R-12, all New. The human elected to fix R-12. R-12: FR1.7's duplicate-serial clause reads 'It is detected from the driver's machine-readable error code SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE' without qualification, while FR1.5 establishes a two-tier design in which the application-level predicate rejects a duplicate before the write and the database's unique index is the backstop. The application predicate is a query, raises no driver error, and therefore cannot be detected from that code, so FR1.7 as written implies the driver code is the only detection path and would remove the mechanism behind FR10.4's inline warning and FR10.5's submit-time block. Qualify the clause to name both tiers: the application-level predicate detects the collision by the shared comparison definition and raises the duplicate-serial error code directly, and the database's unique index raises SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE, which is detected by the same code and never by message text; the pass/fail states that a duplicate caught by either tier surfaces the same duplicate-serial code. No scope change, no new product decision, no reopening of the approved decision set.

---

The application predicate is a query, raises no driver error, and therefore cannot be detected from that code, so FR1.7 as written implies the driver code is the only detection path and would remove the mechanism behind FR10.4's inline warning and FR10.5's submit-time block. Qualify the clause to name both tiers: the application-level predicate detects the collision by the shared comparison definition and raises the duplicate-serial error code directly, and the database's unique index raises SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE, which is detected by the same code and never by message text; the pass/fail states that a duplicate caught by either tier surfaces the same duplicate-serial code. ## Decision Recorded
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T13:08:05Z
**Event**: DECISION_RECORDED
**Stage**: requirements-analysis
**Decision**: Consolidated summary of the Requirements Analysis stage, revision 4: all twelve findings from the three advisory review passes are addressed, with R-11 accepted as an open note. FR1.7 now names both detection tiers explicitly - the application-level predicate detects the collision by the shared comparison definition and raises the duplicate-serial code directly, and the database's unique index raises SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE, detected by the same code and never by message text - and both tiers surface the same code, so the driver code is named without implying it is the only detection path. FR1.5 cross-references FR1.7 on the shared code. Unchanged from the previous revisions: the release boundary, the deferred-versus-out-of-scope split, the retirement record for FR4.5, FR1.2's assigned-to half and FR5.3 in part, and all twenty confirmed answers. No scope change, no new product decision, and no reopening of the approved decision set.
**Options**: Looks correct,Request changes
**Checkpoint**: Consolidated Summary Confirmation
**Questions File**: aidlc/spaces/default/intents/261106-asset-tracker-mvp/inception/requirements-analysis/requirements-analysis-questions.md

## Question Answered
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-09T13:26:26Z
**Event**: QUESTION_ANSWERED
**Stage**: user-stories
**Details**: Q1: A. Two personas; Q2: A. No. A holder is data on an asset, never a persona; Q3: A. Hybrid by capability area; Q4: C. One story per lifecycle family, plus separate stories for the reason vocabulary and for the audit behaviour; Q5: A. One cross-cutting story that states the rule once, with each write story asserting attribution in its own acceptance criteria; Q6: A. Recorded as Won't Have stories with their approved standing intact and a named deferred capability; Q7: A. Yes. The stories state that the actor comes from trusted server-side context, that a write request's actor field is never trusted, and that every audit entry must carry an actor.
